The GREY/BLACK coloring, the shared `path` array (pushed/popped at exactly the same points the recursive version did), and `reportCycle`'s cycle-reporting semantics (D-013: static compile-time graph over permission-to-permission `computedUserset` edges only, `tupleToUserset` edges excluded, the whole permission rejected even with a live non-cyclic branch present) are all preserved exactly, not just approximately.
**A second, latent bug found live while verifying the Bug B fix, and fixed in the same pass:** `reportCycle` called `cycle.join(' -> ')` **inside** its per-member loop, rebuilding the same O(N)-length string N times for an N-member cycle — O(N²) total, invisible before this fix because the recursive `dfs` always stack-overflowed at N≈5,000-10,000, before a cycle that large could ever be fully detected and handed to `reportCycle`. Once the iterative rewrite removed that masking crash, a 20,000-permission adversarial cycle took 33.8 seconds and a 100,000-permission one crashed the process with `FATAL ERROR: ... JavaScript heap out of memory` — a _different_ DoS (CPU/memory exhaustion instead of a stack overflow) that would have left Bug B only partially closed had it gone unnoticed. Fixed by hoisting the `.join(' -> ')` call outside the loop (computed once per cycle, not once per member) — after the fix, the same 20,000-permission cycle rejects in 140ms and a 100,000-permission one in ~800ms with no unusual memory growth; a 300,000-permission cycle (well past anything a 1 MiB request body could even contain) still completes in ~4 seconds. This was found by direct load-testing during verification, not by static reasoning about the code.
**Defense in depth considered, not implemented here:** the audit also suggested a `.max()` on the API's request-body schema and/or a Fastify `bodyLimit` in `src/api/server.ts` — out of scope for this fix (a different file, being addressed in parallel); recommended as a genuinely worthwhile second layer regardless of the structural fixes above, since a smaller body-size ceiling would reject a pathological schema before it ever reaches the parser at all.
**How this was verified:** (1) Both bugs reproduced live, for real, before any fix — exact crash mechanism (`RangeError`, with a captured stack trace for Bug B confirming it originates in `dfs`, never in `parseExpression`/`parseAtom`) and roughly the input size the audit predicted. (2) The iterative `dfs` rewrite was checked for byte-identical output against the original recursive version across 255 hand-built and randomly generated permission graphs (self-loops, 2-cycles and longer N-cycles up to 500 nodes, diamonds, disjoint components, a mixed live/dead-branch case per D-013, a `tupleToUserset` self-reference that must never be flagged, and 240 randomly generated graphs across 4 sizes/many seeds) — zero mismatches, including exact cycle-path message text. (3) New regression tests added (`test/unit/schema/recursion-depth-guards.test.ts`): the paren-nesting ceiling accepted at exactly `MAX_EXPRESSION_NESTING_DEPTH` and rejected one level over, a case well past the audit's reported 3,000-5,000 crash range rejected cleanly rather than throwing, a 10,000-permission legitimate acyclic chain compiling successfully and quickly, and a 10,000-permission adversarial flat cycle (comfortably past the audit's 8,500-9,600 range) rejected cleanly with exactly 10,000 located errors, fast — this new file's 5 tests, and all 22 tests across `test/unit/schema/` as a whole, pass in under half a second total. (4) Fail-check: the fix was reverted via `git stash` (source files only, test file untouched since it's new/untracked), all four new stack-overflow/rejection-boundary tests failed for the right reason (three real, unhandled `RangeError`s; one missing rejection), then restored and confirmed byte-identical via `md5sum` before re-confirming clean. (5) The full existing `test/unit/schema/**` suite and `test/isolation/identifier-and-tuple-validation.fuzz.test.ts` pass unchanged (226 tests total across the whole unit suite, zero regressions, zero database required). (6) `npx tsc --noEmit` (project-wide), `npx eslint`, and `npx prettier --check` all clean against every touched/added file.
**Revisit if:** A future, genuinely legitimate schema needs more than 100 levels of parenthesis nesting (no real schema in this project has ever needed more than 1-2) — reconsider `MAX_EXPRESSION_NESTING_DEPTH` with a concrete case in hand, not preemptively. If the API-layer body-size backstop above is implemented later, cross-reference it here.
**Update (2026-08-16, D-077):** Implemented the same day — D-077 is the API-layer `bodyLimit` backstop this entry's own "Revisit if" clause asked to be cross-referenced once it landed.

## D-068 — An infrastructure failure (exit 3) always prints something to stdout for `--format markdown`/`json`; the tracked PR comment is never silently blanked

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo audit, HIGH finding #8) · **Status:** settled
**Decision:** `soundnessRun`'s (`src/cli/commands/soundness.ts`) `catch` block, and its `DATABASE_URL`-not-set early return, both now go through a new `printInfrastructureFailure(format, message)` helper. For `--format markdown`, it prints `renderSoundnessInfrastructureFailureMarkdown(message)` (new, `src/report/markdown.ts`) to stdout; for `--format json`, it prints `renderSoundnessInfrastructureFailureJsonString(message)` (new, `src/report/json.ts`) to stdout; for `--format text`, stdout is untouched (stderr-only, exactly as before). Both new renderers start with `SOUNDNESS_REPORT_MARKER`, the exact string `decidePrCommentAction` (`src/report/prComment.ts`) matches to decide "update this comment" vs. "post a new one" — omitting it would mean the _next successful_ run creates a brand-new comment instead of updating this failure comment in place, orphaning it. As a second, independent layer, `scripts/post-soundness-comment.mjs` now throws (refusing to call the GitHub API at all, before touching any existing comment) if `soundness-report.md` is empty or whitespace-only — a narrow check on blankness only, never on length, so a genuinely short `sound` verdict is never mistaken for a failure.
**Confirmed the bug first, not just from the audit description:** built the CLI (`npm run build`) and ran `DATABASE_URL="postgres://user:pass@127.0.0.1:1/nonexistent" node dist/cli/index.js soundness run --queries 5 --format markdown > soundness-report.md` against the pre-fix source — `soundness-report.md` was a genuine 0-byte file, `soundnessRun`'s `catch` block having printed only to stderr; exit code was `3`. Re-ran the identical repro after the fix: `soundness-report.md` was 425 bytes, began with `SOUNDNESS_REPORT_MARKER`, and named the real `ECONNREFUSED` error text; exit code unchanged at `3`.
**Widened beyond the literal finding, deliberately, to one more call site in the same function:** the audit description names only `runSoundnessFuzz` throwing from inside the `try` block. `soundnessRun` has a second, structurally identical exit-3 path — the `DATABASE_URL`-not-set early return, a few lines above the `try` — which had the exact same defect (stderr only, empty stdout) for the exact same reason (both are "infrastructure failure," per `src/report/exitCodes.ts`'s own doc comment, and both feed the same `--format markdown` capture). Routing it through the same `printInfrastructureFailure` helper closed it at zero marginal design cost and is covered by its own tests below. Deliberately did **not** widen further to the malformed-`--queries`/`--seed`/`--format` early returns (exit code 2): those are argument-validation failures, a categorically different case from "infrastructure failure," and `.github/workflows/soundness.yml`'s own invocation (`--queries "$SOUNDNESS_FUZZ_QUERIES" --format markdown`, both fixed, always-valid values) can never actually reach them — widening there would be speculative scope growth with no real failure mode behind it.
**Alternative rejected (for the JSON fallback shape):** Reuse `SoundnessJsonReport` with placeholder/zeroed fields (`verdict: 'sound'` or a `null` verdict, `falseGrantCount: 0`, `divergences: []`).
**Why it lost:** A `SoundnessJsonReport` promises fields computed from a real completed run; none of that exists when `runSoundnessFuzz` never got far enough to compute any of it. A consumer checking `report.verdict === 'sound'` against a zeroed report would silently treat an infrastructure failure as a clean pass — precisely the miscategorization this project's own asymmetric-verdict discipline (§6.5, D-006) exists to prevent, just at the JSON-shape level instead of the markdown-copy level. `SoundnessInfrastructureFailureJson` (`{ status: 'infrastructure_failure', message }`) has no `verdict` field to misread; a consumer has to handle it as its own case.
**Whether `--format json` needed a fallback at all, given nothing in this repo consumes it:** decided yes. `src/cli/commands/soundness.ts`'s own top-of-file doc comment claims "stdout is the report and nothing else" for `json` in the same sentence it makes that claim for `markdown` — leaving `json` silently empty on this exact failure would be the same category of dishonesty the `markdown` fix exists to close, just for a consumer this repo doesn't have yet. The cost of closing it now (one more small, pure rendering function, no new dependencies) is low enough that leaving it open on the theory "nothing reads it yet" wasn't worth the asymmetry.
**Whether the workflow YAML itself needed a change:** decided no, beyond none. `.github/workflows/soundness.yml`'s existing `cat soundness-report.md` step already surfaces an empty file's contents (or lack thereof) in the Actions log for a human to notice at the CI-log level, and its "Fail the job if..." step already correctly propagates any non-zero exit code regardless of report content — neither of those was ever the actual bug. The bug was specifically that the _PR comment itself_ (not the job's pass/fail state, not the log) could go silently blank; the fix belongs in the two places that actually decide what that comment's body is (the CLI, and the script that reads its output), not in the workflow orchestration around them.
**Whether the workflow _script_ needed a companion change (the empty-body guard in `post-soundness-comment.mjs`):** decided yes, as narrow defense-in-depth, not redundant with the CLI fix. The CLI fix closes every failure mode reachable through `soundnessRun`'s own two exit-3 call sites; it cannot reach a failure mode outside that function entirely — a crash before `soundnessRun` runs at all, a future regression that reintroduces a silent-stdout path, a disk/redirection problem in the workflow step. A one-line "refuse to act on a blank body" check in the one place that actually calls the GitHub API closes that residual gap for a few lines, and — checking literal blankness only, never a length threshold — cannot misfire on a legitimately short `sound` result the way a naive "is this short" heuristic could have. Verified directly: piped an empty file through the script with fabricated `GITHUB_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_EVENT_PATH` env vars — it threw and exited non-zero _before_ any `fetch` call, confirmed by inspection of the thrown stack (only `requireEnv`/JSON-parsing/the new guard ran; `checkedFetch` never appears in the trace).
**Tests:** `test/unit/cli/soundness-infrastructure-failure.test.ts` (new) — six DB-free-or-real-unreachable-DB tests, matching `test/unit/cli/soundness.test.ts`'s own established style: `runSoundnessFuzz` mocked to reject with a known message for exact-output assertions (markdown, json, text-unchanged), one test against a _real_ unreachable database (unmocked, matching the finding's own repro method) proving the fix holds against a genuine infrastructure failure and not only a mock standing in for one, and two tests covering the `DATABASE_URL`-not-set path specifically (markdown prints a report and never calls `runSoundnessFuzz`; text is unchanged).
**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean; `npx eslint .` clean; `npx prettier --check .` clean (after one `--write` pass on `src/report/json.ts` for a line-wrap it re-flowed); full `npx vitest run` (excludes `*.integration.test.ts`, no Docker needed) — 227/227 passed, up from 221 before this fix's six new tests; real end-to-end smoke test against the built CLI for all three formats × both failure paths (shown above and in this fix's own final report). **Fail-check performed:** reverted `printInfrastructureFailure`'s body to its pre-fix stderr-only behavior (source only, tests untouched), re-ran the new test file — exactly the four markdown/json/real-DB/`DATABASE_URL`-missing-markdown tests failed, for the right reason (`expected "log" to be called 1 times, but got 0 times`); the two text-format tests, correctly unaffected by the revert, stayed green. Restored the file and confirmed byte-identical restoration via `md5sum` before finishing.
**Revisit if:** A future consumer starts actually parsing `--format json`'s output in a script (not just this repo's own workflow) — at that point, `SoundnessInfrastructureFailureJson`'s shape becomes an external contract worth being more deliberate about (e.g., should `status` ever carry more detail than a flat `message` string) rather than the internally-consistent-only shape it is today.

## D-069 — Three depth-budget accounting bugs in the production resolver, one root cause: `resolve()`'s per-call `depth` didn't mean the same thing everywhere it was read

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo-audit follow-up) · **Status:** settled
**What was wrong (three separate, independently-verified defects, all in `src/resolve/production/resolver.ts`'s `resolve`/`evalRewrite`):**

1. **(CRITICAL — false_grant)** `resolve()`'s relation branch called `sqlRelationMembershipWithWitness(ctx.pool, object, name, subject, ctx.maxDepth)` — the full configured ceiling, unreduced by however much TS-level `depth` the walk had already spent reaching that call. `fetchReachableFrontier`'s recursive CTE bounds its own recursion by `m.depth < $4` using that same unreduced value, so a single relation-membership lookup could walk the _entire_ budget on its own, on top of whatever it cost to get there. Live repro (`namespace group { relation member: user | group#member }`, `namespace doc { relation viewer: user | group#member; permission view = viewer }`, a 3-level nested `group:g0→g1→g2` chain with the grant on `g2`, `maxDepth: 3`): `referenceCheck` → `allowed: false`; `productionCheck` → `allowed: true, depth: 3` — production granted a permission with no real path, a `false_grant` per §6.5, not merely a disagreement.
2. **(HIGH — false_deny)** `evalRewrite`'s `tupleToUserset` case called `resolve(ctx, subject, newObject, rule.computedUserset, visited, depth + 1)` — an extra `+1` on top of the `+1` `resolve()`'s own permission branch already spends entering `evalRewrite` (`evalRewrite(ctx, permission.rewrite, subject, object, visited, depth + 1)`), while the sibling `computedUserset` case (`resolve(ctx, subject, object, rule.name, visited, depth)`, depth unchanged) does not. Net cost per hop: `tupleToUserset` = 2, `computedUserset` = 1 — the same conceptual one-hop crossing charged twice for one mechanism and once for the other. Live repro (25-level `folder` parent chain, `permission view = editor | parent->view`, grant on the deepest node, `maxDepth: 25`): reference allows out to 24 hops of distance (denies only at the true ceiling, 25); production started denying around 11–12 hops — roughly half its real capacity, a real `false_deny`.
3. **(found independently while verifying the fix for 1–2, not one of the two originally reported) — a residual off-by-one at the ceiling itself.** `resolve()` denied at `depth >= ctx.maxDepth`; the reference resolver's `resolveMembership` (`src/resolve/reference/resolver.ts`) denies only at `depth > ctx.maxDepth`. Hand-deriving the exact depth cost for every rewrite-rule kind (as the fix's own verification step requires) showed the two engines compute the _identical_ numeric `depth` value at every corresponding node once (1) and (2) are fixed — the only remaining difference was the comparator's strictness. A node reached at depth exactly `maxDepth` must still execute (and can still resolve a direct grant with zero further recursion) on both engines; `>=` rejected it one hop earlier than `>` does. Confirmed live and empirically, not just by derivation: a small folder chain at `maxDepth: 6` denied on production at distance 5 (one hop short of where reference still allows) even after (1) and (2) were fixed.

**Why none of this was caught before:** `test/unit/resolve/production/cross-resolver-agreement.integration.test.ts` (the actual Phase 4/5 cross-resolver exit-criterion suite) had no fixture deeper than 3 levels, and its cyclic-nesting fixtures used a 2-node cycle — nowhere near deep enough to make TS-level `depth` bookkeeping the deciding factor in any assertion. All three bugs are invisible at shallow depth: bug 1 needs `depth` already spent by a prior permission hop before a relation lookup with its own nested userset chain; bug 2's doubling only diverges from the true cost once a chain is long enough that `maxDepth / 2` and `maxDepth` land on opposite sides of the required hop count; bug 3 only bites exactly at the ceiling boundary. A 3-level fixture can't reach any of those conditions.

**The fix** (`src/resolve/production/resolver.ts`): (1) line 400, `const remainingDepth = Math.max(0, ctx.maxDepth - depth);` passed to `sqlRelationMembershipWithWitness` (line 406) instead of `ctx.maxDepth`. (2) lines 514–521, the `tupleToUserset` call to `resolve()` now passes `depth` unchanged, matching `computedUserset`'s own convention (line ~442). (3) line 385, `if (depth > ctx.maxDepth)` instead of `>=`.
**Confirmed the three fixes don't need to interact with `union`/`intersection`/`exclusion`'s own handling:** all three combinators forward `depth` unchanged to their children in both `resolve()`'s caller (`evalRewrite`) and the reference resolver's `evalRewrite` — neither file's combinator cases were touched, and re-deriving the cost for every rewrite-rule kind by hand (union, intersection, exclusion, computedUserset, tupleToUserset, entering a relation's own SQL lookup) after the fix confirmed production's accounting now matches reference's exactly, for every kind, not just the two originally reported.

**Verified, independently, before trusting the fix:**

- Both original repro scenarios reproduced live against real Postgres _before_ the fix (confirming the bug reports were accurate as described) and re-run clean _after_ it.
- Regression tests added to `cross-resolver-agreement.integration.test.ts` (`depth-budget accounting parity — deep fixtures the earlier 3-level fixtures above cannot catch`, 6 new `it`s across 3 sub-`describe`s: the false_grant fixture + its raised-budget control; the 25-level false_deny fixture + its past-ceiling control; the isolated ceiling-comparator fixture + its past-ceiling control).
- **Fail-check, each bug reverted one at a time, the other two left fixed:** reverting (1) alone → exactly one new test fails (`expected true to be false`, the false_grant assertion); reverting (2) alone → two new tests fail (the 25-level fixture _and_ the small ceiling-comparator fixture, since doubling the per-hop cost also exhausts that smaller budget — this fixture is **not** independently isolated from bug 2, and its own in-code comment was corrected to say so plainly rather than overclaim isolation it doesn't have); reverting (3) alone (comparator back to `>=`, with (1) and (2) both still fixed) → the same two tests fail, confirmed as the comparator's own doing specifically. Restored, confirmed byte-identical (`git diff` clean, matching the known-good hash), reran clean.
- Full existing suite unaffected: `npx vitest run` (fast suite) 221/221 passing; `test/unit/resolve/production/*.integration.test.ts` (cross-resolver-agreement, production-check-behavior, production-resolution-path) and the new depth-budget fixtures, 41/41 passing via the project's own LOCALVERIFY-against-real-local-Postgres technique (no Docker in this sandbox).
- The real, standard-budget differential soundness fuzz (`test/isolation/differential-soundness.fuzz.integration.test.ts`, 5,000 queries, real Postgres, real `productionCheck`) run against the fixed resolver: `false_grant: 0`, `verdict: sound`.
- `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check` (targeted files) all clean.

**A genuine coverage gap found while proving the fuzz harness has power against this specific fix, disclosed rather than smoothed over:** per this project's own rule that a clean soundness run must never be reported without also demonstrating the harness catches a real break, bug 1 was reintroduced in the real resolver and run against the _real_ 5,000-query, real-Postgres differential fuzz (`runSoundnessFuzz`), at the literal standard configuration (default `maxDepth` — 25 for production — several fresh random seeds, full 5,000-query budget each). **It did not catch the bug.** The generator (`src/soundness/generators.ts`) caps objects per namespace at `OBJECTS_PER_NAMESPACE_MAX = 6`, so no random graph it produces can contain a userset-subject chain anywhere near the ~20+ hops needed to make "remaining budget" and "full budget" diverge under a ceiling of 25 — the structural precondition for bug 1 to produce a wrong boolean essentially never arises at the generator's own scale. The harness _does_ have real power against this exact bug class: forcing `maxDepth` down to 2 via `runSoundnessFuzz`'s existing `maxDepth` option (shrinking the coincidence window to something the generator's small graphs can actually hit) produced `verdict: unsound` with real, nonzero `false_grant` counts in the large majority of 40 tried seeds; at `maxDepth: 5` it was less reliable but still occasionally caught it. This is a _new_ coverage-gap finding, distinct from D-035 (which is about a latency-only cycle-guard defect structurally invisible to boolean comparison at _any_ depth) — bug 1 is a genuine boolean-level defect that boolean comparison _can_ catch, but the current generator's object-count ceiling makes it statistically invisible at the _standard_ configuration specifically. Fixing the generator (deeper/longer chains, or a dedicated deep-nesting fixture family) is Phase 5/`soundness-engineer` territory, out of scope for this fix, and is flagged here rather than silently left for someone to eventually notice the hard way.
**Alternative considered for reporting this:** stay silent about the gap since the standard 5,000-query run reports clean and the targeted regression tests (which do catch all three bugs, deterministically, every time) are what actually gates this PR.
**Why it lost:** The persona rule this whole fix was done under is explicit — a fuzz harness that has never been observed catching a real bug is not evidence it can catch one, and reporting a clean run without having also demonstrated power for the resolver in question is exactly the failure mode to avoid. The honest state is: the _targeted_ regression tests reliably catch all three bugs; the _standard-configuration_ fuzz run does not reliably catch bug 1's specific shape; a _tightened-depth_ fuzz run does. All three of those are true at once and the report should say so, not average them into "the fuzz harness works."
**Revisit if:** Any future work touches `src/soundness/generators.ts`'s object-count constants or adds a dedicated deep-chain fixture family — at that point, re-run this same probe (several fresh seeds, standard `maxDepth`, a deliberately-broken depth-budget accounting bug) to confirm the gap has actually closed, not just that the constants changed.

## D-070 — A guaranteed, deterministic deep chain closes D-069's own generator coverage gap — and reveals that the gap is only _half_ closable without touching `runner.ts`

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (closes D-069's own "Revisit if") · **Status:** settled

**Decision:** `src/soundness/generators.ts`'s `generateFixture` now hand-builds a second guaranteed structure into every fixture, alongside the pre-existing guaranteed cycle — a **guaranteed deep chain** (`buildGuaranteedDeepChain`), created the same deliberate way (first, in strictly increasing `order`, entirely independent of any randomly drawn edge). It reuses `hierNs`'s existing `parent`/`editor`/`view` shape and `groupNs`'s existing `member`/`view` shape (no new namespace, no new rewrite-rule shape) to build:

- a 24-hop `hierNs` parent chain, `dc_h0..dc_h24` (`DEEP_CHAIN_HIER_HOPS`), with `dc_h0.editor` carrying two real tuples: a plain grant straight to a dedicated witness user, and a userset edge into —
- a 13-group `groupNs` nested-`member` chain, `dc_g0..dc_g12` (`DEEP_CHAIN_GROUP_HOPS_OUTSIDE`), with plain grants at exactly the 12th group (`DEEP_CHAIN_GROUP_HOPS_INSIDE`) and the 13th.

Four reserved queries (indices 2-5, appended after the two pre-existing cyclic reserved queries so their own indices are untouched) are hand-derived against this exact structure:

- **Query 2** (`dc_h13`, `DEEP_CHAIN_ISOLATED_HOP`): a pure `tupleToUserset` chain, no relation-lookup budget involved at all — isolates D-069 bug 2 (each hop's cost doubling from 1 to 2 pushes `2 × 13 = 26` past the ceiling while the correct `13` stays comfortably inside it).
- **Query 3** (`dc_h24`, `DEEP_CHAIN_HIER_HOPS`): the same pure chain at its full length — sits exactly at the TS-level ceiling boundary (`D + 1 <= CHECK_MAX_DEPTH`, i.e. `D <= 24`) — exercises D-069 bug 3's `>=`/`>` comparator (not independently isolated from bug 2 either, matching D-069's own honest disclosure for its analogous fixture).
- **Query 4** (`dc_h12`, `DEEP_CHAIN_INTERMEDIATE_HOP`, "inside" witness, `k = 12`): `D + k = 24`, exactly at the shared `D + k <= CHECK_MAX_DEPTH - 1` boundary — allowed under correctly-scoped accounting.
- **Query 5** (`dc_h12`, "outside" witness, `k = 13`): `D + k = 25`, one hop past that boundary — denied under correctly-scoped accounting at a _shared_ ceiling, but wrongly _allowed_ by D-069 bug 1's unreduced `ctx.maxDepth` budget when the relation-lookup budget isn't actually reduced by the TS-level depth already spent.

Objects reserved for this construct (`dc_h*`/`dc_g*`) are excluded from `assignRandomTuples`'s per-object randomization loop — a set membership check added right before that loop's call site — so no randomly drawn edge can ever attach an extra `parent`/`editor`/`member` tuple to one of them and silently invalidate the hand-derived boundary the reserved queries depend on. `OBJECTS_PER_NAMESPACE_MIN`/`_MAX` are untouched; every other namespace's random object/tuple generation is unaffected.

**Alternative considered: raising `OBJECTS_PER_NAMESPACE_MAX` broadly instead.** Rejected. It would inflate every generated fixture's object/tuple count for a benefit (occasionally producing a long-enough random chain) that is both narrow (only this one depth-accounting property needs it) and _probabilistic_ even at a much higher ceiling — a random chain's real length depends on how many of the randomly assigned `order`-respecting edges happen to actually chain together, which is not the same thing as "guaranteed exactly this long." A dedicated, hand-placed deep-chain family gets the exact boundary values needed (`D + k` landing precisely on both sides of the real ceiling) deterministically, on every single seed, at zero cost to every other namespace's own object/tuple count — the same reasoning D-069's own "Revisit if" already anticipated. `hasUsersetCycle`'s acyclicity guarantee is preserved by inspection (the chain is a hand-built straight-line path, provably acyclic, disjoint from the pre-existing `cycleA`/`cycleB` cycle by object id), not by relying on `assignRandomTuples`'s own `order`-filter rule (which this construct's edges don't go through at all, since they're added directly via `addTuple`, never via `assignRandomTuples`).

**The most important thing this fix's own live verification found — a genuine, load-bearing architectural fact about `runSoundnessFuzz`'s "standard configuration," not a shortfall in the deep chain's own depth:**

D-069 bug 1's `false_grant` **cannot be produced at the literal standard/default `runSoundnessFuzz` configuration (`options.maxDepth` omitted) by any deep-chain construction, at any depth, no matter how the generator is redesigned.** This was proven two ways before it was trusted, not assumed from the fuzz run's own clean result:

1. **By hand-derivation.** At the default configuration, `productionCheck` uses `env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` (25) while `referenceCheck` independently defaults to `DEFAULT_REFERENCE_MAX_DEPTH` (1000, `src/resolve/reference/resolver.ts`) — these are _not_ the same value unless a caller explicitly passes `maxDepth` to `runSoundnessFuzz` (which the standard/CI invocation never does). Bug 1's own worst-case "over-reach" is bounded by roughly production's _own_ nominal ceiling in each of the two places it can bite (the TS-level walk still enforces `depth <= CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` on its own, untouched by bug 1; the relation-lookup's own SQL budget, even unreduced by bug 1, is still capped at the nominal `ctx.maxDepth`, never larger) — so any real chain a _buggy_ production resolver can find is, by construction, at most on the order of `2 × CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` (~50) hops long, trivially within reference's 1000-hop budget. A `false_grant` requires `productionAllowed && !referenceAllowed`; since production `true` always means a _real_ tuple chain exists (the SQL frontier scan only ever follows real `relation_tuples` rows), and that same real, ≤50-hop chain is always independently reachable by reference's own brute-force scan within its 1000-hop budget, reference always _also_ says `true` — agreement, never a divergence, regardless of the bug.
2. **Live, empirically, against real Postgres**, both to catch any error in step 1's derivation and because this project's own standard forbids trusting a derivation over a live check: a hand-built `(D, k)` sweep (`D = 20` fixed, `k = 1..30`, the exact shape query 4/5 above use) was run against the _fixed_ resolver — production denies starting exactly at `k = 5` (`remainingDepth = CHECK_MAX_DEPTH - 21 = 4`), matching the derivation precisely — and then against the resolver with bug 1 _reintroduced_ — production now allows up to `k = 25` (the bug's own unreduced nominal cap) for _every_ `k` in the sweep, and reference allows _all_ of them (`k = 1..30`) throughout, in both the fixed and buggy runs. Not one `false_grant` anywhere in the sweep, in either state — full agreement is the correct-and-buggy-alike outcome at mismatched ceilings.
3. **Confirmed against the real, standard-configuration fuzz run itself**, exactly as this task's own acceptance criterion demands: `runSoundnessFuzz` at the real, unmodified 5,000-query standard budget, real Postgres, `options.maxDepth` omitted, run across 10 fresh seeds with bug 1 reintroduced — **0/10 seeds reported `unsound`; `false_grant` totalled `0` across all 10** (`false_deny` also dropped from its guaranteed baseline of 1 to 0 on every seed — the bug's own extra permissiveness makes query 5 agree with reference instead of diverging from it, exactly per point 1 above). The _same_ 10 seeds, with the fix restored, also report `sound`, `false_grant: 0` (as they must — nothing here claims the fix is wrong, only that this specific gap can't be closed this way).

What _does_ reliably close it: pinning `maxDepth` explicitly, via `runSoundnessFuzz`'s own already-existing `maxDepth` option (`runSoundnessFuzz(pool, { maxDepth: env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH })`) — applying the _same_ ceiling to both resolvers is exactly the condition step 1's derivation identifies as necessary. Run the same way (10 fresh seeds, real Postgres, standard 5,000-query budget, bug 1 reintroduced): **10/10 seeds reported `unsound`, with `false_grant: 1` on every single seed** — deterministic, not probabilistic, a dramatic reliability improvement over D-069's own disclosed "33/40 seeds at `maxDepth: 2`" (a degenerate near-zero-capacity ceiling that barely exercises real schema complexity) or "occasionally, at `maxDepth: 5`". The same 10 seeds with the fix restored report `sound`, `false_grant: 0` on every seed, at the _same_ pinned ceiling — confirming the deep chain does not itself introduce a spurious divergence when both resolvers are correct, at either configuration.

**This is disclosed here exactly as plainly as D-069 disclosed its own gap, per this project's own standing rule** (§0's persona brief; see D-069's own "why it lost" for the same reasoning applied to itself): the deep chain genuinely, reliably, deterministically closes the gap for a _caller who pins `maxDepth`_ — which is a real, already-supported, legitimate way to invoke the harness, not a hypothetical one — but it does **not**, and structurally _cannot_, make bug 1 detectable at the _literal_ default/CI invocation, because that invocation's own two independent depth ceilings (`env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` for production, `DEFAULT_REFERENCE_MAX_DEPTH` for reference) are 40x apart by design. Closing _that_ half of the gap is a `runner.ts`-scoped decision (e.g., defaulting `SoundnessRunOptions.maxDepth` to `env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` rather than leaving it `undefined`, so both resolvers share a ceiling unless a caller deliberately asks for something else) — explicitly out of scope for this fix per its own task boundary ("stay scoped to `src/soundness/generators.ts`"), and flagged here rather than left for someone to discover the hard way, exactly as D-069 itself flagged the generator half of this same gap for `soundness-engineer` territory.

**What the deep chain _does_ close, unconditionally, at the literal default configuration, with no `maxDepth` pinning needed:** D-069 bugs 2 and 3 (both `false_deny`-shaped, not `false_grant`-shaped) are real, boolean-level defects reference always disagrees with regardless of its own generous ceiling — confirmed live: bug 2 reintroduced alone raises `false_deny` from its guaranteed baseline of 1 to exactly 4 on every seed tried (queries 2, 3, and 4 all newly denied, on top of query 5's existing baseline denial); bug 3 reintroduced alone raises it from 1 to exactly 2 (query 3 alone, isolating the ceiling-comparator boundary). Neither flips `verdict` to `unsound` (per §6.5/D-006, `false_deny` never blocks on its own) but both are now a real, deterministic, always-present signal in every run's own report — a _new_ regression a reviewer reading `falseDenyCount` would actually notice, where before this fix neither bug moved that number at all at standard scale.

**Verification performed (live, not just derived):**

- `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check .` all clean.
- `npx vitest run` (fast suite): 276/276 passing (239 pre-existing + 37 new, `test/unit/soundness/generators.test.ts`) — the new file asserts, across 5 distinct seeds, the exact hand-derived tuple/query shape `buildGuaranteedDeepChain` claims to build (every parent edge, every group-chain edge, both plain grants, exactly two tuples on `dc_h0`'s `editor` relation and exactly one on every interior chain node — proving no stray random tuple ever lands on a reserved object), the four reserved queries' exact subject/object/relation shape at indices 2-5, that `referenceCheck` allows all four (confirming they're real, findable chains, not phantoms), that the pre-existing cyclic reserved queries/coverage invariants are untouched, byte-identical reproducibility for a fixed seed, and graceful degradation for `queryCount < 6`.
- `test/isolation/differential-soundness.fuzz.test.ts` (the pre-existing suite, unmodified): 8/8 passing, no assertion needed changing.
- Real, live, standard-configuration `runSoundnessFuzz` (5,000 queries, real Postgres, `dryRun: true`), fixed resolver: 10/10 fresh seeds report `sound`, `false_grant: 0`, `false_deny: 1` (the guaranteed, expected, non-blocking asymmetry from query 5) at default `maxDepth`; 10/10 fresh seeds report `sound`, `false_grant: 0`, `false_deny: 0` at `maxDepth` pinned to `env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` — confirming requirement (2), a correct resolver never sees a spurious divergence from this construct at either configuration.
- The three live probes described above (bug 1 at default: 0/10 unsound; bug 1 at pinned: 10/10 unsound with `false_grant: 1` each; bugs 2 and 3 individually reintroduced at default: deterministic `false_deny` increases to 4 and 2 respectively) — each performed via a direct, temporary, single-line source edit to `src/resolve/production/resolver.ts`, rebuilt, run, and **restored byte-identical (confirmed via `md5sum` matching the pre-edit hash) before the next probe**, never left in place, never committed.
- `test/isolation/differential-soundness.fuzz.integration.test.ts` and `test/unit/resolve/production/cross-resolver-agreement.integration.test.ts` (D-069's own regression suite) both re-run in full via this project's own LOCALVERIFY-against-real-local-Postgres technique (no Docker in this sandbox) against the fixed resolver: 1/1 and 20/20 passing respectively, confirming this change didn't disturb either.
- Timing: every real 5,000-query `runSoundnessFuzz` call above completed in 5-10 seconds (comparable to a pre-existing baseline run timed at the same budget before this change) — the deep chain adds `O(depth)` tuples (~40: 24 parent edges + 2 editor tuples + 12 group-chain edges + 2 plain grants), not `O(depth²)` or worse, and does not measurably change the standard run's wall-clock cost. `differential-soundness.fuzz.integration.test.ts`'s own 600-second timeout is not remotely threatened.

**Revisit if:** `runner.ts`'s default `SoundnessRunOptions.maxDepth` handling changes (e.g., defaulting to `env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` instead of leaving both resolvers at their own independent defaults) — at that point, re-run this same live probe (bug 1 reintroduced, several fresh seeds, the _new_ default configuration) to confirm the literal-default-config gap this entry documents has actually closed, not just that a config knob moved. Also revisit if `env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH`'s documented default (25) or `DEFAULT_REFERENCE_MAX_DEPTH` (1000) ever change — the `DEEP_CHAIN_*` constants are hardcoded relative to the _current_ 25 and would need re-deriving to keep landing exactly on the real boundary (see their own doc comment in `generators.ts`).

## D-071 — `runSoundnessFuzz` resolves one effective `maxDepth` and passes it to both resolvers explicitly, closing D-070's own "Revisit if" — the standard-configuration fuzz run now genuinely catches D-069 bug 1's `false_grant` shape, no `maxDepth` pinning required

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (closes D-070's own "Revisit if") · **Status:** settled

**Decision:** `src/soundness/runner.ts`'s `runSoundnessFuzz` now resolves `const effectiveMaxDepth = options.maxDepth ?? env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH;` exactly once, and `checkAllQueries` (its signature tightened from `maxDepth: number | undefined` to `maxDepth: number`, since a caller can no longer pass "no opinion" down to it) passes that one resolved value to _both_ `referenceCheck` and `productionCheck`, on every query, unconditionally (`{ maxDepth }`, never the old `maxDepth !== undefined ? { maxDepth } : {}` conditional that let each resolver silently fall back to its own default). `options.maxDepth`, when a caller explicitly sets it, still overrides both uniformly, exactly as it always has — that half of the existing contract is unchanged; only the _omitted_ case's behavior changed, from "each resolver quietly does its own, independently mismatched thing" to "both resolvers are held to the one real, production-configured ceiling."

**This is exactly what D-070's own "Revisit if" named**, and this entry closes that loop explicitly: D-070 proved, by hand-derivation and live Postgres testing, that D-069 bug 1's own `false_grant` shape is architecturally undetectable by `runSoundnessFuzz`'s literal default invocation — not because the generator's chains were too shallow (D-070 already fixed that), but because `productionCheck`'s own default (`env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH`, 25) and `referenceCheck`'s own default (`DEFAULT_REFERENCE_MAX_DEPTH`, 1000) are 40x apart, so any real chain a buggy production resolver can find (bounded to roughly `2 x CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` even with the bug) is always independently reachable by reference's own much larger budget too — agreement, never a divergence, regardless of the bug. D-070 explicitly flagged closing this as `runner.ts`-scoped work, out of its own stated boundary. This entry is that work.

**Live-verified, the actual acceptance criterion:** D-069 bug 1 reintroduced (the exact one-line change D-070 itself used — `resolve()`'s relation branch passing `ctx.maxDepth` instead of `Math.max(0, ctx.maxDepth - depth)`), run against the real, standard-configuration `runSoundnessFuzz` (5,000 queries, real Postgres, `options.maxDepth` genuinely omitted — not pinned) across 10 fresh seeds: **10/10 seeds reported `unsound`, with `false_grant: 1` on every single seed** — the identical deterministic result D-070 already established only the `maxDepth`-pinned invocation could reliably produce, now true of the literal default too. The same 10 seeds, resolver restored (confirmed byte-identical via `md5sum` before and after), report `sound`, `false_grant: 0` on every seed at the same, now-unified default — confirming the fix doesn't itself introduce a spurious divergence.

**Design questions worked through:**

- **Does `SoundnessRunOptions.maxDepth`'s own doc comment need updating?** Yes — done. It previously described omitting the option as "production-realistic depth, exactly what real callers see" without saying anything about what the reference resolver did in that case (an omission that, in hindsight, is exactly the gap that let the mismatch go unnoticed for as long as it did). It now states plainly, with a direct D-071 cross-reference, that omitting the option resolves one effective ceiling and gives it to both resolvers — not "each resolver does its own thing."
- **Should `SoundnessRunResult` record which `maxDepth` was actually used?** Considered, decided **no**, for a real reason rather than reflexively: (1) `soundness_runs`'s own persisted schema (`src/store/migrations/0003_soundness_runs.sql`) has no `max_depth` column, and adding one is a real migration, not a one-line addition — a materially bigger change than "resolve a value consistently." (2) Checked whether any existing field already does something like this before considering a new one, per this task's own explicit instruction: `graph_seed` is recorded because it (with `queryCount`, also recorded) is a _required input_ to `generateFixture` itself — without it, the fixture cannot be reproduced at all. `maxDepth` is different in kind: it never affects fixture _generation_ (`generateFixture`'s own signature is `(seed, queryCount)`, nothing more), only the _check_ phase afterward, and — critically — no real invocation path in this repository (`authz soundness run`'s CLI, `.github/workflows/soundness.yml`'s CI trigger, the API) has ever varied it; there is no `--max-depth` CLI flag anywhere, exactly as this task's own framing already observed. In practice, `options.maxDepth` is _always_ omitted outside of test code today, and resolves to whatever `env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` was at call time — a documented, versioned repository default, not a per-run varying input the way `graph_seed`/`queryCount` genuinely are. (3) `renderSoundnessJson`/`SoundnessJsonReport` (`src/report/json.ts`) builds its own explicit field list from `SoundnessRunResult` rather than spreading it — a new field on the result type would not automatically reach either report renderer, meaning "record it" would really mean touching the DB schema, the runner, and both report renderers for a value nothing currently varies. The natural point to add this is if/when a real `--max-depth`-style caller-facing knob is ever added (making `options.maxDepth` a genuinely varying, user-supplied input rather than a hardcoded 25) — see this entry's own "Revisit if."
- **Does D-070's own reserved query 5 (the "outside" combo witness, `D + k = 25`) change shape under the new default?** Yes, checked live rather than assumed: at the _old_ default (mismatched ceilings), query 5 was a guaranteed, expected, non-blocking `false_deny` on every run (production correctly denied at its own real ceiling; reference, given a 1000-hop budget, found the same real chain anyway). At the _new_ default (both resolvers now given `env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH` = 25), reference now denies it too — **confirmed live: `false_deny: 0` on every one of 10 fresh seeds at the new default**, versus the previously-guaranteed `false_deny: 1`. This is not a regression and not a new risk: it is agreement (both `false`), the strictly _more correct_ outcome now that both resolvers share the ceiling the query was always designed to straddle — it never became a `false_grant` risk, because a correctly-scoped production resolver still denies it regardless of what reference does. `src/soundness/generators.ts`'s own doc comments on query 4/5 (immediately above where they're constructed in `generateFixture`) are updated to describe both the old (pre-D-071) and new (post-D-071) behavior accurately, rather than left describing a "mismatched" default that no longer exists.
- **Existing tests that call `runSoundnessFuzz` without an explicit `maxDepth`:** audited every call site (`grep`, not assumed) — `test/unit/cli/soundness.test.ts`, `soundness-format.test.ts`, `soundness-infrastructure-failure.test.ts` all mock `runSoundnessFuzz` itself, never exercising its internal `maxDepth` resolution; `test/unit/api/server.test.ts` likewise; `test/isolation/differential-soundness.fuzz.integration.test.ts` and `test/unit/soundness/dry-run-cleanup.integration.test.ts` call the real function with no `maxDepth` override and assert on `falseGrantCount`/verdict shape, never on a specific `falseDenyCount` value that this change could have broken — both re-run in full against real local Postgres (LOCALVERIFY) and pass unmodified. None needed a change.

**New test:** `test/unit/soundness/runner-maxdepth-resolution.test.ts` (new, DB-free — `publishSchema`/`writeTuple`/`productionCheck` mocked, `generateFixture`/`referenceCheck` real, `referenceCheck` additionally spied-on-but-delegating so its call arguments are observable without changing its behavior, mirroring `runner-dry-run-cleanup-failure.test.ts`'s own established pattern) — two tests: `options.maxDepth` omitted means both resolvers are called with `{ maxDepth: env.CHECK_MAX_DEPTH }` on every query; `options.maxDepth` explicitly set means both are called with that exact value, uniformly, confirming the override contract is unchanged.

**Verification performed:**

- `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check .` all clean.
- `npx vitest run` (fast suite): 278/278 passing (276 pre-existing including D-070's own 37 + 2 new in `runner-maxdepth-resolution.test.ts`).
- The live probe above (bug 1 reintroduced, real standard-configuration `runSoundnessFuzz`, `options.maxDepth` genuinely omitted, 10 fresh seeds): **10/10 `unsound`, `false_grant: 1` each** — the actual acceptance criterion for this fix. The same 10 seeds with the resolver restored: **10/10 `sound`, `false_grant: 0`**. Both confirmed via a temporary, single-line edit to `src/resolve/production/resolver.ts`, rebuilt, run, and restored byte-identical (`md5sum` matching before/after) before moving on.
- A separate live run (10 fresh seeds, fixed resolver, genuinely default `maxDepth`, real Postgres): **10/10 `sound`, `false_grant: 0`, `false_deny: 0`** — confirming requirement (2), no spurious divergence introduced by unifying the default.
- Explicit-override sanity checks (fixed resolver, `maxDepth: 25` and `maxDepth: 2` passed explicitly): both still `sound`, `false_grant: 0`, confirming the pre-existing override contract still works unchanged.
- `test/isolation/differential-soundness.fuzz.integration.test.ts`, `test/unit/soundness/dry-run-cleanup.integration.test.ts`, and `test/unit/resolve/production/cross-resolver-agreement.integration.test.ts` all re-run in full via this project's own LOCALVERIFY-against-real-local-Postgres technique (no Docker in this sandbox): 1/1, 5/5, and 20/20 passing respectively — none needed a change, none broke.
- **Fail-check:** `checkAllQueries`'s `referenceCheck` call temporarily reverted to omit the `{ maxDepth }` options object entirely (source only, reproducing the pre-fix "reference silently uses its own default" behavior) — both new tests in `runner-maxdepth-resolution.test.ts` failed for the right reason (`expected undefined to deeply equal { maxDepth: 25 }` / `{ maxDepth: 7 }`). Restored, confirmed byte-identical via `md5sum` before re-confirming clean.
- Timing: every real 5,000-query `runSoundnessFuzz` call above completed in 5-10 seconds, unchanged from D-070's own baseline — this fix changes which `maxDepth` value is used, not how many queries or tuples are involved, so no new performance concern.

**Revisit if:** A real `--max-depth`-style caller-facing knob is ever added to `authz soundness run` (or any other real invocation path) — at that point, `options.maxDepth` becomes a genuinely varying, user-supplied input rather than a hardcoded 25, and persisting the resolved value on `soundness_runs`/`SoundnessRunResult` (a real migration, plus `src/report/markdown.ts`/`json.ts` updates) becomes worth doing for the same reason `graph_seed`/`queryCount` already are — reopen the "should the result record which maxDepth was used" question above with that concrete need in hand, not preemptively.

## D-072 — Unmatched API routes get their own rate limit and this API's one documented error envelope, via `setNotFoundHandler`

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo audit findings #13, #14, MEDIUM) · **Status:** settled

**What was wrong, confirmed live before any fix:** `@fastify/rate-limit` wires itself into a route exclusively via `fastify.addHook('onRoute', ...)` (confirmed by reading `node_modules/@fastify/rate-limit/index.js` directly) — a hook that never fires for a request matching no registered route, since Fastify's 404 handling runs entirely outside the matched-route hook lifecycle. Two consequences, both reproduced live: 150 consecutive requests to a non-existent path never hit a 429 (a control run against a real route hit 429 at request 101, confirmed again after this fix at request 98 — see this entry's own verification below); and a 404 returned Fastify's own raw default body (`{statusCode, error, message}`), never this API's one documented `{error:{code,message}}` envelope every other response uses — a client written against the documented single-envelope contract would crash trying to parse it.

**The fix:** `app.setNotFoundHandler({ preHandler: app.rateLimit() }, handler)` — `@fastify/rate-limit`'s own documented pattern for exactly this gap (its README's "Preventing guessing of URLs through 404s" section), reusing the global `max: 100, timeWindow: '1 minute'` budget already registered rather than a separate one. The handler itself calls a new `notFoundError()` constructor (`src/api/errors.ts`, report-designer) — a new `not_found` `ApiErrorCode` (404), inserted between `unauthorized`/401 and `rate_limited`/429 to preserve `API_ERROR_STATUS`'s existing ascending-status-code ordering — so an unmatched route now returns the same envelope shape as every other rejection.

**Verified live**, not just by reading the plugin's own README: against a real running server (real local Postgres, `authz serve`), a request to a bogus path returned `404` with body `{"error":{"code":"not_found","message":"not found — no route matches this method and path"}}`; firing 105 sequential requests at distinct bogus paths hit `429` at request 98 (comfortably before the 100-request budget exhausts, consistent with a handful of earlier requests in the same script hitting other routes first).

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check .` all clean. `test-author` added coverage in `test/unit/api/server.test.ts` (a request-loop-until-429 test against a bogus path, and a shape assertion on the 404 envelope) and a `notFoundError` describe block in `test/unit/api/errors.test.ts` — see this branch's own commit for exact pass counts.

**Revisit if:** A future route needs a materially different rate-limit budget than the global default for its own 404 neighbors specifically (nothing in this API's five real operations suggests that today) — `app.rateLimit({...})` accepts an override object per the same README section, if that ever becomes necessary.

## D-073 — `GET /health` never forwards the raw Postgres driver error, and no longer conflates "database unreachable" with "the namespace-listing query itself failed"

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo audit findings #15, #22, #23, MEDIUM) · **Status:** settled

**What was wrong (finding #15):** `/health`'s catch block forwarded `(err as Error).message` verbatim into the wire response — a real Postgres connection failure routinely includes internal hostnames, ports, or usernames, and this route is deliberately unauthenticated (load-balancer/uptime-monitor convention, D-051), so any network caller could read it. `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-051's own "revisit if" clause named exactly this category ("connection pool internals that could aid an attacker profiling the deployment") as a future trigger; it had already arrived.

**What was wrong (finding #22):** One try/catch wrapped both the `select 1` connectivity probe and the downstream `listLatestNamespaceVersions` query. A failure in the second query alone (e.g. an unapplied migration) was reported identically to a genuine connectivity failure (`database.reachable: false`) — a claim that was simply untrue whenever Postgres itself answered `select 1` fine.

**The fix:** Two independent try/catches in `src/api/server.ts`'s `/health` handler. Each failure is logged server-side via `request.log.error` with its own distinct message, and the wire response uses a fixed, generic string (`'database is unreachable'` / `'namespace listing failed'`), never the real driver error. `src/api/responses.ts` (report-designer) gained a new `HealthNamespaceListStatus` type — `{ok:true; namespaces} | {ok:false; error}`, independent of `HealthDatabaseStatus` — so `database.reachable` stays strictly accurate (only ever reflects the connectivity probe) while `namespaces` can independently report its own query's own outcome. `healthResponse` fails closed (503, `unavailable`) on _either_ failure — a credible skeptic in the audit itself argued this is operationally correct regardless of cause, and that argument is accepted here — but the two causes are now honestly distinguishable in the logs and in which field carries the error.

**Finding #23 ("raw Postgres errors also echo through the unauthenticated check/expand routes"), no separate code change:** this finding's own premise — `/check`/`/expand` being unauthenticated — was already closed by D-064 (merged earlier the same day, ADMIN_API_KEY-gates both routes). `infrastructureUnavailableError`'s raw-message behavior on those (and every other gated) route is deliberate and documented (see that constructor's own doc comment in `src/api/errors.ts`): it deliberately mirrors the CLI's own `"Postgres: <message>"` phrasing for a now-_authenticated_ caller, the same trust level as someone holding `DATABASE_URL` access directly. `/health` is the one route in this API that is deliberately unauthenticated by design (D-051) — that's what makes its own raw-message disclosure (finding #15) a real, distinct problem this entry fixes, and what makes #23 moot as literally filed rather than something requiring its own fix.

**Verification:** Live-verified against real local Postgres: a reachable DB with a real namespace list returns `200`/`ok`/the wrapped `{ok:true,namespaces}` shape; an unreachable DB (bad host/port) returns `503`/`unavailable`/`{reachable:false,error:'database is unreachable'}` with no connection-string fragment anywhere in the response body. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check .` clean. `test-author` updated the existing `an-unreachable-pool-produces-503...` test to assert the raw thrown message is absent from the _entire_ response body (not just renamed the expected string) — see that test file for the exact assertion.

**Revisit if:** A future route needs to distinguish more than two downstream-dependency failure causes on one health surface — the two-independent-try/catch pattern here generalizes (one try/catch per distinct dependency call, one status field per outcome), but isn't built as a generic N-dependency abstraction since `/health` only ever had two calls to make.

## D-074 — `assertTokenObserved` rejects a non-integer or negative token before ever querying the database, closing a `NaN`-comparison hazard

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo audit finding #9, MEDIUM) · **Status:** settled

**What was wrong:** `requested > observed` (and every other `>`/`<`/`>=`/`<=` comparison involving `NaN`) evaluates to `false` in JavaScript, never `true` — so `Number('not-a-token')`, `Number(undefined)`, or any other malformed input that resolves to `NaN` silently passed the "has this been observed" check instead of throwing, the exact inverse of what a consistency-token boundary check must do with an input it cannot make sense of.

**The fix:** `assertTokenObserved` now checks `Number.isInteger(requested) && requested >= 0` immediately after coercion, before ever calling `currentToken(pool)` — a malformed token now fails fast, synchronously, with no database round trip. Every _current_ real caller (`src/api/server.ts`'s Zod `atToken: z.number().int().nonnegative().optional()` schema; `src/cli/commands/check.ts`'s own `Number.isInteger(atToken)` guard) already validates before reaching this function, so this never fires against either of today's real call paths — this is a defensive boundary check for `ProductionCheckOptions.atToken`, a public type any future caller could hand a malformed value through directly, not a fix for an observed bug in either existing caller.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check .` clean. `test-author` added `test/unit/store/tokens.test.ts` (new file — no unit coverage existed for this module before, only integration coverage) — DB-free, a fake `Pool` whose `query` throws if actually called proves the new guard short-circuits before any query for `NaN`/non-integer/negative input, plus regression coverage for the pre-existing valid-token behavior.

**Revisit if:** Never for the guard itself. If a future caller ever needs a _reason code_ distinguishing "malformed input" from "token not yet observed" (today both throw a plain `Error` with a human-readable message, matching every other error in this module), reconsider a typed error hierarchy here — not needed today since nothing catches and branches on these two cases differently.

## D-075 — `runSoundnessFuzz` throws a distinguishable `SoundnessFixtureError` for a broken generated fixture, so the CLI can finally tell "generator bug" (exit 2) apart from "infrastructure failure" (exit 3)

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo audit finding #12, MEDIUM) · **Status:** settled

**What was wrong:** `src/cli/commands/soundness.ts`'s `catch` block mapped _every_ error `runSoundnessFuzz` could throw to exit code 3 and a `"Postgres: <message>"`-prefixed report, including the three throw sites explicitly labeled in their own message text as "a generator bug, not a resolver finding" (a broken generated schema failing to compile, a rejected publish, a rejected tuple write). This directly contradicted this same file's own top-of-file exit-code table, which already documented generator/fixture failures as exit code 2 (build spec §7: "insufficient fuzz coverage or a schema/tuple validation failure") — the code and its own doc comment disagreed, and `src/report/exitCodes.ts`'s doc comment independently repeated the same wrong framing (calling a generator bug an example of exit-code-3 territory). Both doc comments are corrected as part of this fix.

**The fix:** `src/soundness/runner.ts` exports a new `SoundnessFixtureError extends Error`, thrown at exactly the three generator-bug sites instead of a plain `Error`. `soundnessRun`'s `catch` block now branches on `instanceof SoundnessFixtureError`: a fixture error sets exit code 2 and calls a new `printFixtureFailure` (never the misleading `"Postgres: "` framing); every other thrown error keeps the pre-existing exit-code-3 / `printInfrastructureFailure` behavior unchanged. `src/report/markdown.ts`/`json.ts` (report-designer) gained `renderSoundnessFixtureFailureMarkdown`/`renderSoundnessFixtureFailureJsonString` — direct siblings of the existing infrastructure-failure renderers, `## FIXTURE_FAILURE` instead of `## INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE`, deliberately never suggesting a database/connectivity problem in their body text.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check .` clean. `test-author` added: `test/unit/soundness/runner-fixture-error.test.ts` (DB-free, confirms `instanceof SoundnessFixtureError` at all three throw sites, mirroring `runner-dry-run-cleanup-failure.test.ts`'s established mocking pattern); CLI-level exit-code-branch tests in `test/unit/cli/soundness.test.ts` (a `SoundnessFixtureError` rejection → exit 2, no `"Postgres: "` in stderr, `FIXTURE_FAILURE`/`fixture_failure` framing in `markdown`/`json` stdout; a plain `Error` rejection → unchanged exit 3 behavior, a regression check the branch didn't break the existing path); new report-renderer tests in `test/unit/report/markdown.test.ts`/`json.test.ts` for the two new render functions.

**Revisit if:** A fourth generator-bug-shaped throw site is ever added to `runSoundnessFuzz` (e.g. a future fixture-validation step) — it must throw `SoundnessFixtureError`, not a plain `Error`, to land in the correct exit-code bucket; nothing enforces this structurally today beyond code review and this decision entry.

## D-076 — Commander's own default usage-error exit code no longer collides with `soundness run`'s security-significant exit code 1

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo audit finding #16, MEDIUM) · **Status:** settled

**What was wrong:** With no `exitOverride()` registered, Commander calls `process.exit(1)` directly from inside `parseAsync` on any usage error (a typo'd flag, a missing argument, an unknown subcommand) — before any subcommand's own `.action()` handler ever runs. Confirmed by reading `node_modules/commander/lib/command.js` directly: every real usage error funnels through `Command.error()`, whose own `exitCode = config.exitCode || 1` default is never overridden with anything else anywhere in that library; the only _other_ exit code Commander itself ever constructs is `0`, for `--help`/`--version` (a non-error display). Exit code `1` is exactly the code `authz soundness run` reserves for "verdict unsound — at least one `false_grant`, always blocking" (§6.5) — a plain CLI usage mistake (`authz soundness run --bogus-flag`) would exit with the identical code a CI script watching for a real, blocking security finding relies on.

**The fix:** `program.exitOverride()` (`src/cli/index.ts`) makes Commander throw a `CommanderError` instead of calling `process.exit` directly; a `try`/`catch` around `await program.parseAsync(process.argv)` catches it and remaps `err.exitCode === 1` to `2` — this project's own established "malformed CLI argument" convention, already used by every command's own argument validation (`soundnessRun`'s `--queries`/`--format` checks, `check.ts`'s `--at-token` check, etc.) — leaving Commander's own `0` (`--help`/`--version`) untouched. Since Commander only ever constructs those two exit codes, this remap is exhaustive: no Commander-originated exit code can reach a caller uncovered.

**Live-verified**, the actual acceptance criterion, by spawning `npx tsx src/cli/index.ts` directly with various arguments: `--help`/`--version` → exit `0` (unchanged); an unknown flag, a missing required argument, and an unknown subcommand → exit `2` (previously `1`); `soundness run --queries not-a-number` → exit `2` (unaffected, this command's own pre-existing validation, not Commander's); `doctor` with no `DATABASE_URL` → exit `3` (unaffected, this command's own infrastructure-failure path, not a Commander usage error).

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check .` clean, plus the live spawns above. `test-author` evaluated adding an automated `child_process`-spawning smoke test (no precedent for this in the repo) — see this branch's own commit/test-author report for whether one was added or the live verification above was judged sufficient on its own.

**Revisit if:** A future Commander upgrade changes which exit codes the library itself constructs (confirmed today, by reading its source directly, to be exactly `0` and `1` — nothing else) — re-verify this remap is still exhaustive against the new version's own `_exit`/`CommanderError` call sites before trusting it.

## D-077 — An explicit, tuned request-body-size ceiling closes D-067's disclosed-but-unimplemented defense-in-depth recommendation; the framework-level error handler now preserves the real HTTP status code instead of flattening every sub-500 error to 400

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (closes D-067's own "defense in depth considered, not implemented here") · **Status:** settled

**Decision:** `src/api/server.ts`'s `Fastify({...})` constructor now sets `bodyLimit: 262_144` (256 KiB) explicitly. Fastify itself already defaults to 1,048,576 bytes (1 MiB) when this option is omitted — confirmed by reading `node_modules/fastify/lib/context.js`/`config-validator.js` directly, not assumed — so this API was never genuinely unbounded; the gap D-067 flagged was that the ceiling was implicit, undocumented, and never tuned to what this API's own five operations actually need, not that one was fully absent. 256 KiB was chosen, not 1 MiB, because every real payload this API legitimately receives is small (a tuple/check/expand body is a few hundred bytes at most; every schema this project has ever written, including `schema/example.authz`, is a few KB) — 256 KiB is generous by roughly two orders of magnitude over any legitimate use while cutting a would-be attacker's usable request-body budget to a quarter of Fastify's own un-tuned default. `schemaSourceBodySchema`'s `source` field additionally gained its own tighter `.max(65_536)` (64 KiB) Zod ceiling — a second, field-specific layer on top of the server-wide one, giving a maliciously oversized schema source its own named Zod rejection rather than relying solely on the blunter whole-body check.

**A necessary companion fix, found while implementing the above, not a separate finding:** `app.setErrorHandler`'s generic branch (`error.statusCode < 500`) previously called `reply.code(resp.status)` — hardcoding `invalidRequestError`'s own `400`, regardless of what the underlying Fastify error's real `statusCode` was. Before `bodyLimit` existed, every real case reaching this branch already _was_ a genuine 400 (a malformed JSON body, an unsupported content type — Fastify's own defaults for both), so this hardcoding changed nothing in practice. Once `bodyLimit` made a `413` (`FST_ERR_CTP_BODY_TOO_LARGE`, "Request body is too large") a reachable case for the first time, silently flattening it to `400` would have discarded a real, standard, distinct status code a well-behaved client or proxy specifically checks for. Fixed by changing that one line to `reply.code(error.statusCode)` — the response _body_ still uses `invalidRequestError`'s shape (no new `ApiErrorCode` is warranted for one framework-level status among several this same envelope already covers), only the wire status code now matches what actually happened.

**Live-verified** against a real running server (real local Postgres): a 300,000-byte JSON body to `POST /schema/compile` returned `413` with `{"error":{"code":"invalid_request","message":"invalid request — Request body is too large"}}`; a 70,000-byte `source` field inside a body still under the 256 KiB server-wide limit returned `400` with the Zod-specific message `"schema source exceeds the 64 KiB limit"` — confirming the two layers fire independently, at the ceiling each is meant to enforce.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check .` clean, plus the live probes above.

**Revisit if:** A real, legitimate use case ever needs a schema source or tuple/check payload larger than these ceilings (nothing in this project's own history has ever come close) — reconsider both numbers together with a concrete example in hand, not preemptively.

## D-078 — A doc-drift sweep: seven stale comments/docs corrected, none changing behavior

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo audit findings #18, #19, #25, #28, #29, #30, #35, all doc-drift, MEDIUM/LOW) · **Status:** settled

Grouped into one entry since each is a small, independent text correction with no behavior change — listed individually for traceability:

- **#18 / `docs/github-governance.md` + `.github/workflows/ci.yml`:** both files' identical stale claim — "a still-`.todo()` integration suite has nothing to fail on yet" as the reason `test-integration` stays advisory — was false since roughly Phase 2 (verified directly: `test/isolation/`'s only remaining `.todo(` matches are historical prose describing tests that _used to be_ `.todo()`, not live skips; 13 real integration test files run today). Both corrected to state the actual, still-current reason: container-based tests carry more infra-flakiness risk than the pure unit suite (a reason `ci.yml`'s own job comment already stated independently) — a flaky testcontainer spin-up failing an unrelated PR's merge is a worse failure mode than the advisory status quo. The underlying governance decision (advisory, not required) is unchanged — only its stated justification was wrong.
- **#19 / `docs/CONSISTENCY.md`:** the doc's own worked SQL example showed `id ... -- the token` and omitted the `operation` `check` constraint — conflating `id` and `token` as one column, in a document whose entire point is explaining why they're deliberately separate (see D-0xx on `write_log.token` as a generated column, referenced from `src/store/migrations/0001...sql`'s own comment). Synced to the real migration's exact column set; added a short paragraph explaining _why_ `token` is a separate generated column rather than `id` reused directly, matching the reasoning already on record in the migration file's own comment.
- **#25 / `src/report/exitCodes.ts`:** a doc comment said `src/cli/commands/soundness.ts` "currently inlines this mapping... it is expected to be refactored to call `soundnessExitCode`" — but that refactor had already shipped in the same commit that added the comment. Corrected to describe present-tense reality.
- **#28 / `README.md`:** the CLI reference table's `authz soundness run` line omitted `--format text|markdown|json`, a real, documented flag. Added.
- **#29 / `schema/example.authz` + `docs/screens/namespaces.html`:** an identical comment in both files used the invalid, hyphenated identifier `group:eng-backend#member` (hyphens are illegal under this DSL's identifier grammar — see D-059) where the real demo data correctly uses `eng_backend` throughout. Both comments corrected to the valid form; no functional schema or demo data was ever wrong, only the comment's own example text.
- **#30 / `docs/DECISIONS.md` (D-020):** D-020's own "Revisit if" ("Phase 4's production resolver settles on its own return shape — confirm then whether the two need to agree on a resolution-path shape") was satisfied by D-036 (Phase 6), which explicitly says "the case D-020 explicitly deferred" — but D-020 itself carried no forward pointer to that resolution, unlike the established D-060→D-063 cross-reference precedent. Added an "Update" note to D-020 closing the loop explicitly.
- **#35 / `vitest.config.ts`:** the coverage-threshold comment said "src/ is currently just Phase 0's env loader, and the isolation suite is intentionally all `.todo()`" — stale since roughly Phase 2 (src/ now has 33 files across all 9 build-spec phases). Corrected to state the real current numbers and, explicitly, that _not_ restoring numeric thresholds here is still a deliberate, separate decision (this config only ever measures the fast unit suite, never the isolation suite, which `--exclude`s `*.integration.test.ts`) rather than an accidental leftover — worth making with real coverage numbers in hand, not as a side effect of fixing a stale comment.

**Verification:** `npx prettier --check .`, `npx eslint .` clean on every touched file; no `.ts`/`.sql` behavior changed, so no test suite impact expected or observed.

## D-079 — Two `package.json` dependency-hygiene fixes: `@types/node` re-pinned to the actual supported Node floor, `testcontainers` dropped as a redundant direct devDependency

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo audit findings #36, #37, LOW) · **Status:** settled

**#36:** `@types/node` was pinned to `^26.2.0` while `engines.node` requires `>=22` and CI runs Node 22 — four majors of drift meant TypeScript would silently accept a Node 26-only API with no way to catch the mismatch until it actually ran on Node 22 in production. Currently benign (confirmed: no such API is actually used anywhere in `src/`, `tsc --noEmit` stayed clean after the repin) but a live landmine for the first contributor who reaches for one. Re-pinned to `^22.20.1` (the latest available 22.x release at fix time) — matches the real supported floor exactly.

**#37:** `testcontainers` was a direct devDependency alongside `@testcontainers/postgresql`, which already depends on it (`npm ls testcontainers` confirmed a single deduped instance, pulled in transitively). Nothing in `test/`/`src/` imports `testcontainers` directly — confirmed by grep — only `@testcontainers/postgresql`. Removed the redundant direct entry; `@testcontainers/postgresql` alone is sufficient and already pins the same version range.

**Verification:** `npm install` (regenerates `package-lock.json`, an 8-line diff — no unrelated dependency churn), `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npx prettier --check .`, and a real `npm run build` all clean after both changes. `npm ls testcontainers @types/node` confirmed the expected resolved tree (one deduped `testcontainers@12.1.0`, `@types/node@22.20.1` resolved everywhere including every transitive consumer).

**Revisit if:** A future Node LTS bump changes `engines.node`'s own floor — re-pin `@types/node` to match at the same time, not as an afterthought.

## D-080 — `publishOne` closes a real concurrent-publish race with a transaction-scoped advisory lock; `deletePublishedNamespaceVersion`'s single-caller contract is now ESLint-enforced, not just documented

**Date:** 2026-08-16 · **Phase:** post-9 (full-repo audit findings #26, #27, LOW/improvement) · **Status:** settled

**#27 — what was wrong:** `publishOne` (`src/schema/publish.ts`) computed a namespace's next version via `select coalesce(max(version), 0) + 1 ... where namespace = $1`, then inserted it — a plain read-then-write race. Two concurrent `publishSchema` calls targeting the same namespace could both read the same `next_version` before either inserted; the loser's `INSERT` then died on `namespace_configs`'s own unique constraint, surfacing as a raw, unhandled Postgres error out of `publishSchema` instead of that function's own `{ ok: false, errors: [...] }` shape.

**The fix:** `await client.query('select pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($1))', [config.namespace])` as the first line of `publishOne`, before the version read — serializes every `publishOne` call for a given namespace string; a concurrent second call blocks before it ever reads `max(version)`, never racing it. `hashtext` (int4) chosen over `hashtextextended` (bigint): the namespace population this locks is small and admin-curated, so a hash collision costs at most a harmless spurious lock wait between two unrelated namespaces, never a correctness bug. `pg_advisory_xact_lock` releases automatically at `COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK` (Postgres's own documented behavior for transaction-scoped advisory locks), matching `publishSchema`'s existing transaction wrapping with no separate unlock call needed. Two alternatives considered and rejected: `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` (no row exists yet on a namespace's very first publish — can't close the race for exactly the case that matters most); catch the unique-constraint violation and retry (works, but adds a real bounded-retry/error-discrimination loop for a narrow, already-low-probability, admin-gated race, where the advisory lock makes it structurally impossible instead). Multiple namespaces published together each take their own lock, one per loop iteration, in the order they appear in the source DSL text — a theoretical cross-publish deadlock (two different concurrent multi-namespace publishes acquiring the same two namespaces' locks in reversed order) is judged negligible and left undefended: Postgres's own deadlock detector would abort one side with an ordinary error, the same failure class the existing `ROLLBACK` path already handles, not a hang.

**#27 — verified live**, the actual acceptance criterion, against real local Postgres: with the fix, two concurrent `publishSchema()` calls against the same brand-new namespace both succeeded with sequential versions `[1, 2]`, repeated independently by both the fix's author and, separately, by the main agent's own from-scratch verification script — 3/3 and 3/3 clean runs respectively. With the fix temporarily reverted, the identical scenario reproduced the raw `23505` unique-constraint-violation 3/3 times, confirming the test is meaningful, not a false pass.

**#26 — what was wrong:** `deletePublishedNamespaceVersion`'s doc comment already stated plainly it exists for exactly one caller (`src/soundness/runner.ts`'s dry-run cleanup) and must not be reached for general "unpublish a namespace" use — but nothing structural stopped a future caller from importing and calling it anyway (real but narrow: requires a future developer to both ignore the doc comment and reach for a function whose name says not to).

**The fix:** A new ESLint flat-config block (`eslint.config.js`) scoped to `files: ['src/**/*.ts']`, `ignores: ['src/soundness/runner.ts']`, using `no-restricted-imports`'s `patterns` option (`group: ['**/schema/publish.js', 'publish.js']`, `importNames: ['deletePublishedNamespaceVersion']`) — any future non-`runner.ts` caller now fails `npx eslint .`, not just code review. `patterns` (glob over the import specifier text) chosen over `paths` (exact string) because every current importer uses a different `../` depth; the glob matches all of them uniformly. Deliberately scoped to `src/**` only, not `test/**`: `test/unit/soundness/runner-dry-run-cleanup-failure.test.ts` legitimately spies on this exact export via a namespace import, and `no-restricted-imports` flags any namespace import unconditionally once one of its names is restricted — the risk this closes is a future _production_ caller, not a test mocking the one sanctioned one.
